5. End-to-end workflows

The nine key flows of SPEC §3.4. In the diagrams solid arrows are synchronous calls (thin = the reply), dashed teal arrows are Kafka events, a purple box is an alt / loop frame. Step numbers match the list under each diagram.

F1 — Customer onboarding (Rahim, assisted by agent Mitu Store)

Rahim walks into the agent shop Mitu Store. The agent registers him (tier 0, wallet P), captures his KYC for tier 2, compliance screens him, and two back-office staff approve. Only then does his wallet become A (active).

  1. POST /v1/customers (msisdn, consents) — Registration trusts that the MSISDN was verified upstream (OTP is deferred). The agent is recorded as assistedBy.
  2. POST /v1/ledger/accounts (accountId = walletId, CUSTOMER_WALLET) — The ledger account is created first (ADR-05). If ledger-service is down the call answers 503 DEPENDENCY_UNAVAILABLE and the provisioning-retry job finishes the job later.
  3. 200 OK — ledger-service answers result: OK (or EXISTS on a retry — both mean success); the wallet becomes READY.
  4. CUSTOMER_REGISTERED, WALLET_UPSERTED (P, tier 0) — Two events go to the outbox in the same database transaction as the new customer and wallet rows.
  5. 201 customerId, walletId, walletStatus P — The client answer: the customer is REGISTERED, the wallet is P (pending KYC, tier 0).
  6. POST kyc-submissions (targetTier 2), PATCH fields, POST documents — Rahim (with the agent) creates a DRAFT submission for tier 2, saves fields (save & resume for 7 days) and uploads NID front/back and a selfie.
  7. POST /v1/kyc-submissions/{submissionId}:submit — Submit runs tier validation, de-duplication, the blacklist check and the risk score (Rahim: 5 points → LOW).
  8. GET /v1/blacklist/check (NID valueHash, MSISDN valueHash) — The blacklist check uses an HMAC valueHash, never the clear NID or MSISDN. If limits-risk is down the submission still proceeds with blacklistCheck = PENDING; the review gate blocks until it is re-checked.
  9. KYC_SUBMITTED (eventId stored on the submission) — The envelope eventId of KYC_SUBMITTED is stored on the submission; it is the correlation key for screening.
  10. screen the customer — compliance-service consumes customer.events and screens the submitted name, NID hash and DOB.
  11. case.events SCREENING_COMPLETED CLEAR (sourceEventId = stored eventId) — The result carries sourceEventId. Only a result whose sourceEventId equals the stored eventId changes the submission (BR-W-24).
  12. delivered: submission SUBMITTED → PENDING_REVIEW — CLEAR without EDD, blacklist done and no open exception → the review gate passes (BR-W-23).
  13. POST :decide APPROVE tier 2 (OPS_MAKER) — An OPS_MAKER reviews the documents and proposes APPROVE; this creates a KycSubmission / APPROVE_KYC approval.
  14. POST /v1/approvals/{id}:approve (OPS_CHECKER) — A different person with role OPS_CHECKER approves. The engine re-checks the gate in the same transaction.
  15. tier 2, risk LOW, wallet P → A (version 8813), after-commit Lua CAS — Approval effects in one transaction (BR-W-26): profile copied, tier 2, risk LOW, wallet base PENDING_KYC → ACTIVE, version + 1. After commit the Redis status cache field becomes 8813:A:2:C:0:0.
  16. KYC_APPROVED, KYC_TIER_CHANGED, RISK_GRADE_CHANGED, WALLET_UPSERTED — Four events are written by the outbox. Notification of approval is done by admin-service rules on customer.events.

F2 — Immediate fraud / AML freeze with write-through

compliance-service freezes Karim's wallet for an AML case. Because the caller is SERVICE compliance-service with AML_FREEZE, it is applied immediately (no maker-checker) and Redis is updated before the HTTP answer.

  1. POST /v1/wallets/{walletId}/status-changes BLOCKED AML_FREEZE ref CASE-2026-000045 — The request names the target, the reason and the case reference; the comment from compliance is only FREEZE <reference> (no narrative).
  2. tx: restriction, wallet A→F v8813, history, outbox wallet.events + audit.events — One transaction: a FULL_BLOCK restriction row, the wallet row (effective status recomputed by StatusResolver, version + 1), a status-history row and two outbox rows.
  3. EVALSHA cas ws:106094 v8813 value 8813:F:1:C:0:0 → 1 — After commit, the write-through runs the Lua compare-and-set (CAS) script. It writes only if the stored version is older. Up to 3 attempts (0 / 20 / 50 ms).
  4. 200 outcome APPLIED, walletStatus F, walletVersion 8813 — For AML / fraud freezes the API answers after the write-through attempt, so a payment service reading Redis already sees F.
  5. WALLET_UPSERTED version 8813 (seconds later) — The outbox relay publishes the event; the cache loader (same jar, cache-loader profile) follows wallet.events.
  6. EVALSHA cas → 0 (already applied) — The CAS returns 0 because version 8813 is already stored. Duplicates and out-of-order events are harmless.

F3 — Closure with same-person payout and the failure path

Karim asks to close his wallet and move the remaining money to another wallet he owns (OWN_WALLET). Staff run the request, a checker approves, and a job finishes it after a 15-minute settle window.

  1. POST /v1/wallets/{walletId}/closures (payout OWN_WALLET toWalletId) — The request records how identity was verified (e.g. NID_PHYSICAL_CHECK) and the payout preference.
  2. GET /v1/ledger/holds + GET /v1/ledger/accounts/{id}/balance — Pre-checks (BR-W-40): open disputes, ledger holds, regulatory freezes, pending approvals, balance. If ledger is unreachable the check is UNKNOWN, which blocks.
  3. 201 state PENDING_APPROVAL (eligible payout target) — The balance does not block because the target wallet passes the same-person rule (same ownerId or same nidHash). A WalletClosure / CLOSE approval is created.
  4. POST /v1/approvals/{id}:approve (checker) — A checker (SUP_CHECKER or OPS_CHECKER, not the maker) approves.
  5. base → PENDING_CLOSURE (Q), settle_after = now + 15 min, SETTLING — The wallet enters Q (no new credits, debits only by the closure payout). The prior base status is remembered for a possible revert.
  6. holds and balance re-check — The final check repeats holds and freezes and reads the current balance.
  7. GET /v1/limits/wallets/{toWalletId}/usage (maxBalancePoisha) — The target wallet's maximum balance is passed to the ledger as constraints.destinationMaxBalancePoisha.
  8. POST /v1/ledger/commands/post-closure-payout (commandId payout:1) — Ledger command 17 moves the balance. commandId = UUIDv5(closureId, "payout:" + attempt) and is persisted before the call, so a retry can never pay twice.
  9. POST /v1/ledger/accounts/{id}:close — Success (or EXISTS = already done) → close the ledger account.
  10. base CLOSED (Z), msisdn_release +180 d, WALLET_CLOSURE_COMPLETED — Wallet Z; the MSISDN cools for 180 days; customer CLOSED if no other live wallet; SMS template WALLET_CLOSURE_COMPLETED to the MSISDN.
  11. restore prior base, AWAITING_ZERO_BALANCE, REVERTED; next try payout:2 — A definitive REJECTED (e.g. MAX_BALANCE_EXCEEDED) reverts the closure; the attempt number goes up by one only now.
  12. GET /v1/ledger/commands/{commandId} next run, else same commandId — Unknown outcome: look the command up first; if not posted, re-send the same commandId and body.

F4 — Cache rebuild (DB_THEN_KAFKA)

If Redis is flushed or a new cluster is built, the cache loader refills it from a database snapshot and then catches up from Kafka. The CAS rule makes the overlap harmless.

  1. t0 = pg_last_xact_replay_timestamp() — Remember how fresh the replica snapshot is.
  2. SELECT tuple WHERE wallet_id > last ORDER BY wallet_id LIMIT 10000 — Scan only READY wallets in wallet-id order (4 parallel range workers).
  3. pipelined EVALSHA cas (newer wins) — Each value is written with the Lua CAS, so a newer value written meanwhile by the API is never overwritten.
  4. offsetsForTimes(t0 − 5 min), seek all partitions — Rewind Kafka to 5 minutes before the snapshot time so no change can be missed.
  5. follow topic, pipelined CAS per poll batch — Keep following the compacted topic (this is also the normal steady-state job of the loader).
  6. HSET wsmeta:loader state READY — Mark the cache as READY in wsmeta:loader (diagnostics only for readers).

F5 — Partner submission, screening and approval chain

A sales officer onboards the agent Mitu Store. The submission is blacklist-checked, risk-graded and screened by compliance, then walks through the approval chain SALES → COMPLIANCE → OPERATIONS.

  1. POST /v1/partner-applications + documents + key persons — A DRAFT application with form data, documents (encrypted at rest) and key persons (NID encrypted + HMAC).
  2. POST /v1/partner-applications/{applicationId}:submit — Submit checks the document checklist, duplicates, blacklist, then allocates the partner code (e.g. A000045674, Luhn check digit).
  3. GET /v1/blacklist/check (valueHash per identifier) — Partner MSISDN, every key-person NID and MSISDN and every bank account are checked by valueHash. Any failure of the call → 503 and nothing changes.
  4. listed false — Any listed: true would answer 422 RISK_BLOCKED BLACKLISTED instead.
  5. risk grade, stages (EDD if HIGH), partner PENDING_SCREENING v1 — Risk score by rules (Mitu Store: RETAIL +10 → LOW). The partner row is created now so screening and audit have a stable partnerId.
  6. PARTNER_UPSERTED PENDING_SCREENING, approval.events stage 1, audit — Stage 1 becomes PENDING (an approval request); later stages wait (WAITING, not published).
  7. partner.events — compliance-service screens every new PENDING_SCREENING version (key persons, legal and display name).
  8. case.events SCREENING_COMPLETED PARTNER CLEAR (sourceVersion 1) — Correlation is by sourceVersion (= the PENDING_SCREENING version) or sourceEventId.
  9. partner → PENDING_APPROVAL, PARTNER_UPSERTED version 2 — CLEAR moves the partner on; the COMPLIANCE and final stages may not be approved before this (SCREENING_NOT_CLEAR).
  10. stage approvals SALES, COMPLIANCE, OPERATIONS via /v1/approvals/{id}:approve — Each stage is approved by a different person with the stage role; the decider must not be the initiator.
  11. last stage approved → application APPROVED, activation row CREATE_WALLET — The activation saga starts in the same transaction (see F6).

F6 — Activation saga (merchant Dhaka Bazar)

After the last approval the partner module activates Dhaka Bazar: it creates the merchant wallet in-process (which calls the ledger), generates the static QR and finalises. Each step is persisted so a crash simply resumes.

  1. createWallet(MERCHANT, partnerId, M, msisdn, categoryCode 3101, DHAKA BAZAR) — WalletFacade.createWallet is a Java call inside the same service (no HTTP). It is idempotent on (ownerType, ownerId, walletType).
  2. POST /v1/ledger/accounts (MERCHANT_WALLET) — The wallet module creates the ledger account first.
  3. walletId, status A — Partner wallets start A (active) with kycTier = 2.
  4. provisioning pending, step stays CREATE_WALLET, retried — If the ledger is down the activation step stays at CREATE_WALLET and ActivationRetryJob retries (backoff 30 s × 2^n, max 15 min).
  5. step GENERATE_QR (static QR) — Merchants and agents get one static Bangla QR; distributors and institutions skip this step.
  6. one tx: partner ACTIVE, wallet_id set, version + 1 — FINALISE is one transaction: status = ACTIVE, reason ONBOARDING_APPROVED, application ACTIVATED.
  7. PARTNER_UPSERTED ACTIVE, PARTNER_ACTIVATED notification, audit — The SMS / in-app template PARTNER_ACTIVATED goes to the new partner wallet.

F7 — Future-dated hierarchy change (Mitu Store moves to a new sub-distributor)

Head office decides that from 1 November the agent Mitu Store reports to a different sub-distributor. The change is approved now and applied by a job at the effective time.

  1. POST /v1/partners/{partnerId}/hierarchy-changes effectiveFrom 2026-10-31T18:00Z — Validated at request time: new parent ACTIVE, no cycle, depth ≤ 4, pair allowed by the hierarchy rules, not backdated, at most one SCHEDULED change.
  2. 202 approvalId — Maker-checker: the change waits for a checker.
  3. POST /v1/approvals/{id}:approve — The checker approves; the approval handler re-validates in the same transaction.
  4. re-validate, insert SCHEDULED, close current at effectiveFrom — A SCHEDULED assignment row is inserted; the current ACTIVE row gets effective_to. (18:00Z is midnight in Dhaka.)
  5. assignment ACTIVE, recompute ancestors of the subtree (chunks of 500) — At the effective time the apply job switches the rows and recomputes ancestor_ids, ancestor_wallet_ids and depth for the partner and every descendant.
  6. PARTNER_UPSERTED per affected partner (effectiveFrom 2026-10-31T18:00:00.000Z) — Every affected partner gets version + 1 and one PARTNER_UPSERTED (so commission and limits services see the new chain).

F8 — QR decode during a merchant payment

Rahim scans the dynamic QR shown by Dhaka Bazar for 350 Tk. The app asks wallet-service to decode it; wallet-service trusts only its database, never the text inside the QR.

  1. POST /v1/qr/decode payload — The app sends the raw EMVCo string it scanned.
  2. TLV parse, CRC check, GUID ours, lookup reference, compare payload hash — Strict TLV parsing and CRC-16 check. Our GUID BD.COM.BITSPAY in tag 26 means "on-us". Dynamic QRs are found by reference (tag 62.05) and the SHA-256 of the whole payload must equal the stored hash, so an edited amount with a recomputed CRC is caught (TAMPERED).
  3. onUs true, payable true, merchantWalletId, amountPoisha 35000, qrPayloadId — Names, wallet ids and the amount come from the database. 35000 poisha = 350.00 Tk.
  4. merchant payment: merchantWalletId, amountPoisha, merchantReference, qrPayloadId — wallet-service does not move money; the payment service does, using the decoded fields.

F9 — Partner suspension by compliance (atomic wallet restriction)

compliance-service suspends the merchant Dhaka Bazar for an AML reason. The partner status and the wallet block commit together, in one transaction, because both modules live in the same service.

  1. POST /v1/partners/{partnerId}/status-changes SUSPENDED AML_FREEZE — SERVICE compliance-service with AML_FREEZE / FRAUD_FREEZE / RISK_PERFORMANCE and target SUSPENDED is applied immediately (BR-PAR-42).
  2. begin tx, partner ACTIVE → SUSPENDED, version + 1 — The partner row changes inside an open transaction.
  3. changeStatus(walletId, BLOCKED, PARTNER_SUSPENDED) — In-process facade call with Propagation.REQUIRED: it joins the same transaction.
  4. restriction FULL_BLOCK, wallet A → F, outbox wallet.events — The wallet module adds the restriction and writes its own outbox row. If it fails, everything rolls back together (BR-PAR-47).
  5. outbox partner.events, audit.events, notification.requests, commit — All outbox rows of the change commit at once.
  6. after outer commit, Lua CAS — Redis is written only after the outer transaction commits, so it never shows a rolled-back state.
  7. 200 partner detail, status SUSPENDED — compliance relies only on partnerId, status and version of the answer.

6. State machines

Rounded boxes are states, a black dot is the start, a double border marks a terminal state. Numbers on the arrows refer to the transition table under each diagram.

Wallet base status

A wallet has a base status (its lifecycle stage) and a set of restrictions layered on top. The single-letter effective status that everyone sees is computed from both (BR-W-02). This diagram shows the base status only.

#TransitionWhen
1[start] → PENDING_KYCCustomer registered (tier 0), reason WALLET_CREATED.
2[start] → ACTIVEPartner wallet created by the activation saga (tier 2).
3[start] → DORMANTPre-registered salary / G2P recipient (reason PRE_REGISTRATION, flag 4).
4PENDING_KYC → ACTIVEKYC approved and tier ≥ minTierForActive (1).
5DORMANT → ACTIVEPre-registration KYC approved, or a REACTIVATE status change approved.
6ACTIVE → DORMANTDormancy job (365 days inactive) or a SET_DORMANT change approved.
7PENDING_KYC → PENDING_CLOSUREClosure approved.
8ACTIVE → PENDING_CLOSUREClosure approved.
9DORMANT → PENDING_CLOSUREClosure approved.
10PENDING_CLOSURE → ACTIVEFinal check failed, payout rejected or closure cancelled: the stored prior_base_status is restored (PENDING_KYC, ACTIVE or DORMANT; the SPEC diagram draws only the ACTIVE case).
11PENDING_CLOSURE → CLOSEDLedger account closed. Terminal; the MSISDN cools for 180 days.

Restriction overlay (BR-W-02)

base \ restrictionsnoneDEBIT_BLOCKCREDIT_BLOCKDEBIT+CREDIT or FULL_BLOCK
PENDING_KYCPPFF
ACTIVEAXCF
DORMANTDDFF
PENDING_CLOSUREQQQQ
CLOSEDZZZZ

Lifting a restriction never "restores" a remembered status: the effective code is simply recomputed from the base and the restrictions that remain. Example: Karim has a KYC_EXPIRED debit block and an AML freeze; lifting the AML freeze leaves him at X, not A.

KYC submission

One customer has at most one open submission. It is created as a DRAFT, submitted, screened, reviewed by a maker and approved by a checker.

#TransitionWhen
1[start] → DRAFTCreate for a targetTier (1 or 2).
2DRAFT → DRAFTSave fields, upload documents; each save extends resumeExpiresAt by 7 days.
3DRAFT → EXPIREDResume window passed (draft-expiry job). Documents of never-submitted drafts are purged after 30 days.
4DRAFT → CANCELLEDCancel.
5DRAFT → SUBMITTEDSubmit with valid data; emits KYC_SUBMITTED.
6DRAFT → EXCEPTIONSubmit with a duplicate NID or a blacklist hit.
7SUBMITTED → PENDING_REVIEWCorrelated screening CLEAR, no EDD, gates pass.
8SUBMITTED → ON_HOLDPOTENTIAL or CONFIRMED match, or eddRequired.
9SUBMITTED → EXCEPTIONA blacklist event matches, or screening is overdue (24 h).
10EXCEPTION → SUBMITTEDOVERRIDE approved (re-emits KYC_SUBMITTED).
11EXCEPTION → REJECTEDResolution REJECT (applies immediately).
12ON_HOLD → PENDING_REVIEWSCREENING_DECIDED CLEAR.
13ON_HOLD → REJECTEDSCREENING_DECIDED REJECT with trigger ONBOARDING (no maker-checker: compliance already used four eyes).
14PENDING_REVIEW → PENDING_APPROVALMaker :decide.
15PENDING_APPROVAL → APPROVEDChecker(s) approve APPROVE_KYC.
16PENDING_APPROVAL → REJECTEDChecker(s) approve REJECT_KYC.
17PENDING_APPROVAL → PENDING_REVIEWChecker rejects the proposal, the approval expires, or the gate failed at approval time.
18SUBMITTED → CANCELLEDCancel.
19ON_HOLD → CANCELLEDCancel.
20EXCEPTION → CANCELLEDCancel.

Closure request

A closure request starts in one of three states depending on the pre-checks, then goes through approval, a settle window, an optional payout and the ledger close.

#TransitionWhen
1[start] → PRECHECK_FAILEDA blocking pre-check fails (dispute, hold, freeze, pending approval or UNKNOWN).
2[start] → AWAITING_ZERO_BALANCEOnly the balance is not zero.
3[start] → PENDING_APPROVALAll checks pass (approval WalletClosure / CLOSE created).
4PRECHECK_FAILED → PENDING_APPROVAL:recheck passes.
5PRECHECK_FAILED → AWAITING_ZERO_BALANCE:recheck, only the balance still fails.
6AWAITING_ZERO_BALANCE → PENDING_APPROVAL:recheck passes (e.g. Karim cashed out his 425.75 Tk).
7PENDING_APPROVAL → SETTLINGChecker approves; wallet becomes Q, settle window 15 min.
8PENDING_APPROVAL → REJECTEDChecker rejects.
9SETTLING → PAYING_OUTWindow over, checks pass, balance > 0 and an eligible OWN_WALLET target.
10SETTLING → CLOSINGWindow over, checks pass, balance 0.
11SETTLING → AWAITING_ZERO_BALANCEFinal check fails; wallet restored (WALLET_CLOSURE_REVERTED).
12PAYING_OUT → CLOSINGLedger command 17 answered OK or EXISTS.
13PAYING_OUT → AWAITING_ZERO_BALANCEPayout REJECTED; wallet restored; next attempt uses payout:2.
14CLOSING → CLOSEDLedger close OK; wallet Z.
15PRECHECK_FAILED → CANCELLEDCancel.
16AWAITING_ZERO_BALANCE → CANCELLEDCancel.
17PENDING_APPROVAL → CANCELLEDCancel (the approval is withdrawn → CANCELLED).
18PRECHECK_FAILED → EXPIREDOlder than 30 days (closure-expiry job).
19AWAITING_ZERO_BALANCE → EXPIREDOlder than 30 days.

Partner application

The onboarding application of an agent, merchant, distributor or institution. SUBMITTED is only a transient value inside the submit transaction and is not drawn.

#TransitionWhen
1[start] → DRAFTCreate (staff, a distributor for its subtree, or a micro-merchant self-registering).
2DRAFT → IN_REVIEWSubmit: blacklist OK, partner row created, stages built.
3DRAFT → EXPIREDdraftExpiryDays (30) without submit.
4DRAFT → WITHDRAWNWithdraw.
5IN_REVIEW → RETURNEDReturn by the role of any PENDING stage.
6RETURNED → IN_REVIEWResubmit (at most maxResubmissions = 3); new round from stage 1.
7RETURNED → WITHDRAWNWithdraw.
8RETURNED → EXPIREDdraftExpiryDays passed.
9IN_REVIEW → REJECTEDStage reject, screening REJECT or a blacklist event.
10IN_REVIEW → WITHDRAWNWithdraw.
11IN_REVIEW → APPROVEDLast stage approved (screening CLEAR).
12APPROVED → ACTIVATINGActivation saga started in the same transaction.
13ACTIVATING → ACTIVATEDFINALISE step done.
14ACTIVATING → REJECTEDOps reject while the activation is BLOCKED (e.g. WALLET_MSISDN_TAKEN).

Each round has stages: WAITING → PENDING → APPROVED | AUTO_APPROVED | RETURNED | REJECTED, and WAITING → SKIPPED when the stage condition is false (e.g. the EDD stage for a LOW-risk applicant).

Partner

The partner row exists from the first submit. Only ACTIVE partners may move money (a float return is also allowed from PENDING_TERMINATION).

#TransitionWhen
1[start] → PENDING_SCREENINGApplication submitted (version 1, PARTNER_UPSERTED).
2PENDING_SCREENING → PENDING_APPROVALCorrelated screening CLEAR.
3PENDING_APPROVAL → PENDING_SCREENINGResubmit with screened fields changed, or :rescreen.
4PENDING_SCREENING → REJECTEDReject, screening REJECT or blacklist.
5PENDING_APPROVAL → REJECTEDReject or blacklist.
6PENDING_APPROVAL → ACTIVEActivation FINALISE.
7ACTIVE → SUSPENDEDStaff (maker-checker), SERVICE compliance (immediate), an ancestor distributor (≤ 7 days) or a blacklist match with auto-suspend.
8SUSPENDED → ACTIVEStaff (maker-checker), the ancestor lifts its own suspension, or the until time passes.
9ACTIVE → PENDING_TERMINATIONStaff (maker-checker); requires no active descendants; all QR codes revoked.
10SUSPENDED → PENDING_TERMINATIONStaff (maker-checker); no active descendants.
11PENDING_TERMINATION → ACTIVEStaff cancels the termination.
12PENDING_TERMINATION → TERMINATED:complete-termination with ledger balance 0 and holds 0.

Hierarchy assignment

Each partner has effective-dated assignments to a parent. A Postgres exclusion constraint prevents overlapping periods.

#TransitionWhen
1[start] → SCHEDULEDA future-dated change is approved.
2[start] → ACTIVEAn immediate change, or the first assignment created at first submit.
3SCHEDULED → ACTIVEHierarchyApplyJob at effectiveFrom.
4SCHEDULED → CANCELLEDCancelled before due, or re-validation failed at apply time.
5ACTIVE → CLOSEDSuperseded by the next assignment.

QR code

Static and dynamic Bangla QR codes. Static codes live until revoked; dynamic codes also expire (default 900 s).

#TransitionWhen
1[start] → ACTIVEGenerated (static or dynamic).
2ACTIVE → REVOKEDRevoke, regenerate (static), display-name change, partner termination or outlet closed.
3ACTIVE → EXPIREDDynamic QR past expiresAt (DynamicQrExpiryJob; purged 90 days later).

Approval request (shared maker-checker engine)

One engine and one table (approval_request) serve both modules. Partner application stages are approval rows too (level = stageNo).

#TransitionWhen
1[start] → WAITINGA partner stage that is not due yet (not published on approval.events).
2[start] → PENDINGCreated (emits APPROVAL_REQUESTED).
3WAITING → PENDINGThe previous stage is APPROVED / AUTO_APPROVED / SKIPPED.
4WAITING → CANCELLEDThe round was closed by a return or reject.
5PENDING → PENDINGAn intermediate level approved (e.g. level 1 of a 2-level HIGH-risk KYC).
6PENDING → APPROVEDLast level approved; the module handler applies the change in the same transaction.
7PENDING → REJECTEDA checker rejects.
8PENDING → RETURNEDA partner stage is returned (published as REJECTED).
9PENDING → EXPIRED72 hours passed (APPROVAL_EXPIRED); partner stage requests are re-issued automatically.
10PENDING → CANCELLEDMaker withdrawal (closure cancel, application withdraw / return).

7. Events

Every event uses the kernel envelope (eventId, eventType, eventVersion, occurredAt, producer = "wallet-service", aggregateType, aggregateId = Kafka key, correlationId, data) and is written through the outbox in the same transaction as the change.

Produced (contracts/provided/events)

TopiceventTypeWhenNotes for consumers
wallet.eventsWALLET_UPSERTEDOnce when a wallet becomes READY, then on every committed change of any wallet field (status, restriction add/lift, tier, category, flags, MSISDN, display name, language). Never for a non-READY wallet; NOOP emits nothing.Compacted, key = walletId. version is strictly +1 per change and equals the cache version — apply only if newer. transaction-, compliance-, pricing-, finance-ops-service and our own cache loader read it.
customer.eventsCUSTOMER_REGISTEREDRegistration, pre-registration (reason PRE_REGISTRATION) and the perf seed.Only type that carries referralCode.
customer.eventsKYC_SUBMITTED:submit, resubmission, or an approved exception override.Identity fields are the submitted values so compliance can screen before approval; riskGrade is the grade computed for this submission. Its eventId is the screening correlation key.
customer.eventsKYC_APPROVEDFinal approval of a submission (reason KYC_APPROVED or KYC_UPGRADE).Carries the new tier and kycExpiryDate (review due date).
customer.eventsKYC_REJECTEDFinal rejection: checker approves REJECT_KYC, screening REJECT, or exception REJECT.reasonCode is the rejection code (e.g. IMAGE_UNCLEAR).
customer.eventsKYC_TIER_CHANGEDThe approved tier changed.targetTier is null.
customer.eventsRISK_GRADE_CHANGEDFirst grade at KYC approval, then every approved regrade (reason RISK_REGRADE).compliance and limits use it for monitoring.
customer.eventsCUSTOMER_PROFILE_UPDATEDApproved profile update, language change, nominees, KYC review status DUE / OVERDUE.reasonCode ∈ PROFILE_UPDATED, PREFERENCES_UPDATED, NOMINEES_UPDATED, KYC_REVIEW_DUE, KYC_REVIEW_OVERDUE.
partner.eventsPARTNER_UPSERTEDAny change of a partner field: submit, screening CLEAR, activation, reject, suspend / reactivate / terminate, category, territory, profile, key persons, risk grade, hierarchy (the partner and every descendant), rescreen.The only type on the compacted partner.events. All 20 fields every time. :republish re-emits without a version bump.

customer.events is not compacted (7 days retention) and has no version: order per customer by offset / occurredAt. The seven types share one data shape.

Topics we write but do not own (admin-service)

Topic / eventTypeWhat we put there
notification.requests / NOTIFICATION_REQUESTEDOnly our 17 templates: WALLET_CLOSURE_REQUESTED, WALLET_CLOSURE_APPROVED, WALLET_CLOSURE_COMPLETED, WALLET_CLOSURE_REVERTED, WALLET_DORMANCY_WARNING, KYC_REVIEW_DUE, KYC_REVIEW_OVERDUE, PARTNER_APPLICATION_RECEIVED, PARTNER_APPLICATION_RETURNED, PARTNER_APPLICATION_REJECTED, PARTNER_ACTIVATED, PARTNER_SUSPENDED, PARTNER_REACTIVATED, PARTNER_TERMINATED, AGREEMENT_EXPIRING, TRADE_LICENCE_EXPIRING, PARTNER_APPLICATION_SLA_BREACHED. Closed wallets are addressed by MSISDN only.
approval.events / APPROVAL_REQUESTED, APPROVAL_DECIDED, APPROVAL_EXPIREDEvery approval transition of both modules (module = wallet | partner). WAITING rows are not published; a returned partner stage is published as REJECTED.
audit.events / AUDIT_RECORDEDEvery state-changing API and job, every document view / download. No NID, account numbers or document bytes; MSISDN / e-mail masked.

Consumed (SPEC §6)

ProducerTopic / eventTypeWhat wallet-service does
compliance-servicecase.events
SCREENING_COMPLETED		Wallet module (subjectType CUSTOMER): applied only if sourceEventId = the open submission's stored KYC_SUBMITTED eventId, else recorded only. CLEAR ∧ ¬eddRequired → PENDING_REVIEW (if gates pass); POTENTIAL / CONFIRMED match or eddRequired → ON_HOLD. For ACTIVE customers only screening_status is updated. Partner module (subjectType PARTNER): applied if sourceVersion (or sourceEventId) matches the latest PENDING_SCREENING emission; CLEAR → partner PENDING_APPROVAL + PARTNER_UPSERTED; a match → POTENTIAL_MATCH (COMPLIANCE stage blocked); on an ACTIVE partner a non-onboarding match sets flag AML_WATCH.
compliance-servicecase.events
SCREENING_DECIDED		After compliance's four-eyes review. Wallet: DECIDED CLEAR → PENDING_REVIEW; DECIDED REJECT (trigger ONBOARDING) → submission REJECTED (SCREENING_REJECT) + KYC_REJECTED without maker-checker; pep_flag set only on decision CLEAR ∧ eddRequired ∧ a PEP match. Partner: DECIDED REJECT (ONBOARDING) → application and partner REJECTED (SCREENING_REJECTED); on an ACTIVE partner → immediate suspension, wallet blocked in the same transaction.
compliance-servicecase.events
RISK_REGRADE_RECOMMENDED		(R2, wallet) Same grade → audit only; else a CustomerRiskGrade / CHANGE_GRADE approval (maker compliance-service, checker CAMLCO or COMPLIANCE_OFFICER); one pending per customer, a newer recommendation supersedes. Approval → grade updated, edd_required, review date shortened, RISK_GRADE_CHANGED. Unknown customer → ignored and counted.
support-servicesupport.events
DISPUTE_OPENED		Upsert one open_dispute row per walletId (kind DISPUTE) — blocks closure pre-check OPEN_DISPUTE.
support-servicesupport.events
DISPUTE_RESOLVED		Delete the dispute's rows.
support-servicesupport.events
REVERSAL_REQUESTED		Upsert rows of kind REVERSAL_REQUEST (also block closure).
support-servicesupport.events
REVERSAL_COMPLETED		Delete the request's rows (any terminal outcome).
limits-risk-serviceblacklist.events
BLACKLIST_ENTRY_UPSERTED		Both modules (separate consumer groups). Wallet: an active entry matching an open submission's NID / MSISDN hash or HMAC(walletId) → submission EXCEPTION BLACKLIST_HIT; matching a live customer → exception BLACKLIST_EXISTING_CUSTOMER (no automatic status change). Partner: matching a live partner (MSISDN, key person NID / MSISDN, bank account, WALLET, PARTNER) → match row, flag BLACKLIST_MATCH, Ops notified; blacklist.autoSuspend → immediate SUSPENDED, else a Partner / SUSPEND approval; pending applications → REJECTED (BLACKLISTED).
transaction-servicetxn.events
TRANSACTION_COMPLETED		Dormancy activity: last_activity_at of fromWalletId (and toWalletId if configured) via an idempotent GREATEST upsert, batched every second, at most one DB write per wallet per day. txnType = REVERSAL ignored. No inbox row.
admin-servicerefdata.events
REFDATA_UPSERTED		Projections for validation: wallet module GEO, REASON_CODE, SYSTEM_PARAM RETENTION.CUSTOMER_KYC; partner module GEO (chain, riskFlag, qrCity), MCC (group → categoryCode, riskLevel), REASON_CODE, BANK. Only a higher version is applied.
wallet-service (self)wallet.events
WALLET_UPSERTED		Cache loader (group wallet-service.status-cache-loader): Lua CAS of the six-value tuple into ws:{bucket}. No inbox table — the version CAS is the idempotent effect.

Other types on these topics (e.g. CASE_OPENED, TICKET_CREATED) are ignored and counted in bitspay_consumer_events_ignored_total. Each consumer: inbox row + effect in one transaction; 5 retries then <topic>.dlq; malformed JSON goes to the DLQ immediately.

8. Error codes cheat-sheet

Errors are application/problem+json bodies with a kernel code, an optional reason (owned by this service, listed in codes.yaml problemReasons) and optional extension members.

Kernel codes used by this service

codeHTTPMeaning
VALIDATION_FAILED400The request is malformed: a bad header, parameter or body field, or a business validation rule failed. The errors[] array lists each problem.
ACTOR_NOT_ALLOWED403The caller (actor type, role, or SERVICE name) may not do this — or lacks authority for this reason code.
NOT_FOUND404The entity in the path does not exist, is not READY yet, or is not visible to this actor.
IDEMPOTENCY_CONFLICT409The same Idempotency-Key was sent again with a different body.
STATE_CONFLICT409The entity is in a state that does not allow this call. The reason member says which rule (see the reasons table).
MAKER_CHECKER_VIOLATION422The checker is the maker, decided an earlier level / stage, or the decider is the application initiator.
RISK_BLOCKED422An identifier is on the blacklist (reason BLACKLISTED). No list details are returned.
SERVICE_DISABLED422The feature is switched off (self-registration) or not available in R1 (bank settlement).
DEPENDENCY_UNAVAILABLE503A dependency this call needs (database, ledger-service, limits-risk-service) did not answer. Read the operation notes for what was kept.

Problem extensions

nametypewhen
changeRequestIduuidreason = ALREADY_PENDING (the existing pending request)
activeDescendantsint32reason = HAS_ACTIVE_DESCENDANTS
ledgerBalancePoishaint64 (poisha)reason = BALANCE_NOT_ZERO
heldPoishaint64 (poisha)reason = BALANCE_NOT_ZERO
existingIduuidreason = OPEN_SUBMISSION_EXISTS | CLOSURE_OPEN (the existing open entity)
missingItemsarray<string>400 on partner application submit (missing documents / key persons / bank account / site visit); errors[] lists the same

The QR decoder's 400 uses errors[] messages from code set qrDecodeError: CRC_MISMATCH, BAD_TLV, MISSING_TAG, BAD_FORMAT. A decodable but not payable QR is not an error: it answers 200 with payable: false and a notPayableReason (EXPIRED, REVOKED, PARTNER_NOT_ACTIVE, UNKNOWN_ACCOUNT, TAMPERED, OFF_US_NOT_SUPPORTED, CURRENCY_NOT_SUPPORTED).

9. Data model at a glance

One Postgres schema svc_wallet; two Flyway locations run in order: db/migration/wallet (also hosts the shared-kernel tables) then db/migration/partner (tables prefixed partner_). No foreign key crosses the modules — the partner's wallet_id is a plain uuid.

Wallet module

Table(s)Purpose
customerOne row per person; NID only as nid_enc (AES-GCM) + nid_hash (HMAC) + last 4; tier, risk grade, screening status, review due date. Unique live MSISDN and NID.
walletOne row per wallet (= ledger account id). base_status, effective status_code, tier, category, flags, provisioning_state, version. Unique (owner_type, owner_id, wallet_type) and live MSISDN.
wallet_restrictionActive / lifted restrictions: kind, reasonCode, reference, source actor.
wallet_flag_holderWho holds which flag bit (per-holder model).
status_change_request, wallet_status_historyRequested and applied status changes; full history with version and approvers.
kyc_submission, kyc_document, kyc_submission_eventSubmissions (state, data, risk, screening correlation id), encrypted document metadata, the onboarding audit trail.
screening_result, exception_itemResults from case.events; the exception queue.
nominee, consent_recordNominees (ID encrypted) and versioned consents.
closure_request, msisdn_release, open_disputeClosures incl. payout attempt / commandId; MSISDN cooling; projection of support.events.
wallet_activity, refdata_item, prereg_batch, config_documentDormancy projection of txn.events; refdata projection; pre-registration idempotency; versioned config JSON.
outbox, processed_event, approval_request, idempotency_record, shedlock, job_runShared kernel tables (one outbox for both modules, one approval engine).

Partner module

Table(s)Purpose
partnerPartner master: code, type / subType, names, MSISDN hash, MCC, category, wallet_id, parent, materialised ancestor_ids / ancestor_wallet_ids, depth, territory, status, risk, version (= PARTNER_UPSERTED version).
partner_application, partner_application_stageApplications (form JSON, chain, screening, risk) and one stage row per round (linked to approval_request).
partner_key_person, partner_document, partner_bank_account, partner_site_visitKYB evidence; NID / account numbers encrypted + HMAC; documents envelope-encrypted.
partner_hierarchy_assignmentEffective-dated parent assignments with a GiST exclusion constraint (no overlap).
partner_activationActivation saga step (CREATE_WALLET, GENERATE_QR, FINALISE, DONE, BLOCKED) and retry data.
partner_qr_codeStatic and dynamic QR rows: payload, payload_sha256, reference, amount, status, expiry.
partner_status_history, partner_risk_flag, partner_blacklist_projection, partner_blacklist_matchHistory, informational flags, blacklist projection and matches.
partner_merchant_outlet, partner_user, partner_agreement, partner_expiry_alert_logR2 records.
partner_config_param, partner_category_code_map, partner_hierarchy_level_def / _rule, partner_approval_chain_def / _stage, partner_document_checklist, partner_risk_score_rule, partner_refdata, partner_processed_eventTyped configuration (maker-checker), refdata projection, partner inbox.

Redis keys (cache cluster)

KeyTTLContent
ws:{bucket}noneHash; field = base64url of the 16 uuid bytes, value = version:status:tier:type:category:flags, e.g. 8813:A:2:C:0:0. bucket = unsigned murmur3_x86_32(uuid bytes) mod 131072. Written only by the Lua CAS; never deleted (closed = Z).
wsn:{walletId}60 sNegative cache written by readers after a 404; we only DEL it when the wallet becomes READY.
wd:{walletId}6 hDisplay data cache (name, masked name, type, status, MSISDN); deleted on every wallet change.
wlk:{walletTypeCode}:{msisdnHashHex16}6 hMSISDN lookup → walletId (no clear MSISDN in the key).
wsmeta:loadernoneLoader state (EMPTY / LOADING / READY), mode, counts, requested.

Retention: customer and KYC data relationship + 5 years (R2 anonymisation job, legal holds checked first); partners not deleted in R1; dynamic QR purged 90 days after expiry; outbox 3 d, inbox 14 d, idempotency 7 d.

10. FAQ and gotchas for new developers

Which calls are idempotent, and how?

Three mechanisms. (1) Idempotency-Key (kernel K3.3): required on POST /v1/customers and POST /v1/customers:preRegister, optional on status-changes, profile updates, closures, partner submit, hierarchy / category / territory / profile changes, bank accounts and QR generation; the stored response is replayed for 7 days. (2) Natural idempotency: POST /v1/wallets on (ownerType, ownerId, walletType); a status change that is already in effect answers NOOP. (3) Deterministic ids: the closure payout commandId = UUIDv5(closureId, "payout:" + attempt).

Why is the wallet still 404 right after I created it?

Ledger-first provisioning: until the ledger account exists the wallet is LEDGER_PENDING and invisible to readers (404, notFound), and no WALLET_UPSERTED is published. If ledger-service was down you got a 503; the provisioning-retry job finishes it. Repeat the create call — you get the same walletId.

Can I read the status from Redis instead of calling the API?

Payment services do exactly that (data contract wallet-status-cache v1): pipelined HGET for sender and receiver; on a miss call GET /v1/wallets/{walletId}/status with single-flight and a circuit breaker; on a 404 you may write wsn:{walletId} for 60 s; if both Redis and the API are down, refuse with 503 — never guess. Only wallet-service writes ws:*.

How fresh is the cache? Can Redis show an old status?

The API writes Redis right after the database commit (3 attempts). If that fails, the cache loader catches up from wallet.events within seconds, and the sampler compares DB and Redis every minute. Because of the version CAS, Redis can be briefly behind but never shows a rolled-back or older state. For AML / fraud freezes the HTTP answer waits for the write-through.

The event arrived before / after the HTTP answer — which one do I trust?

Events are eventually consistent (outbox relay, seconds). A caller confirms its own freeze or lift from the HTTP outcome (APPLIED / NOOP), not from wallet.events. Consumers of wallet.events / partner.events must apply a message only if its version is greater than the stored one, because redelivery and replay are normal.

Does wallet-service move money or know balances?

No. It never talks to TigerBeetle and never changes a balance. It asks ledger-service to create / close accounts, reads balances and holds for closure pre-checks and partner termination, reads batch balances for the unclaimed report, and sends exactly one money command: 17 post-closure-payout. Balances are never cached.

What happens if ledger-service times out during a closure payout?

The outcome is UNKNOWN. On the next closure-settle run the service first calls GET /v1/ledger/commands/{commandId}; if not posted it re-sends the same commandId and body. The attempt number (and so the commandId) changes only after a definitive REJECTED. It never closes on an unknown balance.

Why do I get 202 instead of 200?

202 means maker-checker: the change was recorded as pending and an approval was created (approvalId in the answer). It takes effect when a different person with a checker role calls POST /v1/approvals/{id}:approve. Only a small table of SERVICE / SYSTEM callers and reasons is applied immediately (BR-W-11, BR-PAR-42).

My status-change returned NOOP — is that an error?

No. The same restriction (wallet, kind, reason, reference) is already active, or a lift found nothing to lift. Nothing changed, the version did not move and no event was emitted.

Why did the wallet version go up although the status letter did not change?

Every applied restriction add or lift bumps the version and updates statusReasonCode, even when the letter stays the same (e.g. an AML freeze on a wallet already F for a customer request). Flags, category, tier, contact and language changes also bump the version but keep the reason.

How do the two modules talk to each other?

Only in-process, through WalletFacade / PartnerFacade and immutable DTO records. Never the other module's tables, entities or clients (ArchUnit test). Category, contact and suspension changes join the partner transaction (atomic); wallet creation during activation runs outside it (it calls the ledger), so activation is a persisted saga.

Where does a customer get notified?

Approval / rejection / blocked messages are sent by admin-service notification rules reading customer.events / wallet.events. wallet-service itself sends only its 17 templates (closure, dormancy warning, re-KYC, partner lifecycle) through notification.requests. Nothing is ever sent for AML_FREEZE / REGULATORY_INSTRUCTION / COURT_ORDER (tipping-off).

How should I handle PII in logs and tests?

Never log NID, full MSISDN, bank account numbers, document bytes, decrypted KYC data or dynamic QR payloads. Masked MSISDN looks like 8801711****00. NID and account numbers are stored only encrypted + HMAC; blacklist checks always send valueHash. A log-scan test enforces this.

Why can a KYC approval fail at the checker with GATE_NOT_SATISFIED?

The review gate is re-checked inside the approval transaction: correlated CLEAR screening, blacklist done (not PENDING / LISTED), no open exception. If, for example, a blacklist event arrived meanwhile, the submission goes back to the matching state. KYC is never auto-approved.

A screening result arrived but nothing changed. Why?

Results are applied only when correlated: for customers sourceEventId must equal the eventId of the latest KYC_SUBMITTED; for partners sourceVersion must equal the latest PENDING_SCREENING version. A late result for an older attempt is recorded only. After 24 h without a result a SCREENING_OVERDUE exception is raised.

How do I test jobs locally?

Use the internal operations: POST /v1/internal/wallet-jobs/{jobName}:run and POST /v1/internal/jobs/dormancy:run (local / dev only), GET /v1/internal/cache/check, POST /v1/internal/cache:rebuild. The local profile points HTTP clients at WireMock (stubs in docs/stubs/) and event fixtures live in docs/fixtures/events/.

Where are the business numbers (365 days, 180 days, 15 minutes…)?

In configuration, not code: wallet config documents (WALLET_PARAMS, KYC_TIERS, RISK_MATRIX, MASKING_POLICY, REASON_AUTHORITY) and partner config sections. All seeds are provisional and change through maker-checker without a redeploy.

Generated from docs/SPEC.md v6 and docs/contracts (contractVersion 1.0.0). If anything here disagrees with the contracts, the contracts win.
